\index{fs.h@{fs.h}!positive\_aop\_returns@{positive\_aop\_returns}}
\index{positive\_aop\_returns@{positive\_aop\_returns}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{positive\_aop\_returns}{positive\_aop\_returns}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{interfaceenum}{enum}} \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_af67fdcdb86f910a84cd6df11231eaaea}{positive\+\_\+aop\+\_\+returns}}}

enum positive\+\_\+aop\+\_\+returns -\/ aop return codes with specific semantics

@\+A\+O\+P\+\_\+\+W\+R\+I\+T\+E\+P\+A\+G\+E\+\_\+\+A\+C\+T\+I\+V\+A\+TE\+: Informs the caller that page writeback has completed, that the page is still locked, and should be considered active. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} VM uses this hint to return the page to the active list -- it won\textquotesingle{}t be a candidate for writeback again in the near future. Other callers must be careful to unlock the page if they get this return. Returned by writepage();

@\+A\+O\+P\+\_\+\+T\+R\+U\+N\+C\+A\+T\+E\+D\+\_\+\+P\+A\+GE\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} A\+OP method that was handed a locked page has unlocked it and the page might have been truncated. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} caller should back up to acquiring a new page and trying again. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} aop will be taking reasonable precautions not to livelock. If the caller held a page reference, it should drop it before retrying. Returned by readpage().

address\+\_\+space\+\_\+operation functions return these large constants to indicate special semantics to the caller. These are much larger than the bytes in a page to allow for functions that return the number of bytes operated on in a given page. \begin{DoxyEnumFields}{Enumerator}
\raisebox{\heightof{T}}[0pt][0pt]{\index{AOP\_WRITEPAGE\_ACTIVATE@{AOP\_WRITEPAGE\_ACTIVATE}!fs.h@{fs.h}}\index{fs.h@{fs.h}!AOP\_WRITEPAGE\_ACTIVATE@{AOP\_WRITEPAGE\_ACTIVATE}}}\mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_af67fdcdb86f910a84cd6df11231eaaeaa2a83a4e533e54a55f5cea94ce4aa7d3d}\label{linux_2fs_8h_af67fdcdb86f910a84cd6df11231eaaeaa2a83a4e533e54a55f5cea94ce4aa7d3d}} 
A\+O\+P\+\_\+\+W\+R\+I\+T\+E\+P\+A\+G\+E\+\_\+\+A\+C\+T\+I\+V\+A\+TE&\\
\hline

\raisebox{\heightof{T}}[0pt][0pt]{\index{AOP\_TRUNCATED\_PAGE@{AOP\_TRUNCATED\_PAGE}!fs.h@{fs.h}}\index{fs.h@{fs.h}!AOP\_TRUNCATED\_PAGE@{AOP\_TRUNCATED\_PAGE}}}\mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_af67fdcdb86f910a84cd6df11231eaaeaa338d1cad9dbbd9ea8f9ec3d6dbfe52b1}\label{linux_2fs_8h_af67fdcdb86f910a84cd6df11231eaaeaa338d1cad9dbbd9ea8f9ec3d6dbfe52b1}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\_\_filemap\_fdatawrite\_range()}{\_\_filemap\_fdatawrite\_range()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} \+\_\+\+\_\+filemap\+\_\+fdatawrite\+\_\+range (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} $\ast$}]{mapping,  }\item[{loff\+\_\+t}]{start,  }\item[{loff\+\_\+t}]{end,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}}}]{sync\+\_\+mode }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\+\_\+\+\_\+filemap\+\_\+fdatawrite\+\_\+range -\/ start writeback on mapping dirty pages in range @mapping\+: address space structure to write @start\+: offset in bytes where the range starts @end\+: offset in bytes where the range ends (inclusive) @sync\+\_\+mode\+: enable synchronous operation

Start writeback against all of a mapping\textquotesingle{}s dirty pages that lie within the byte offsets $<$start, end$>$ inclusive.

If sync\+\_\+mode is W\+B\+\_\+\+S\+Y\+N\+C\+\_\+\+A\+LL then this is a \char`\"{}data integrity\char`\"{} operation, as opposed to a regular memory cleansing writeback. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} difference between these two operations is that if a dirty page/buffer is encountered, it must be waited upon, and not just skipped over.

Return\+: \%0 on success, negative error code otherwise. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\index{fs.h@{fs.h}!\_\_generic\_file\_write\_iter@{\_\_generic\_file\_write\_iter}}
\index{\_\_generic\_file\_write\_iter@{\_\_generic\_file\_write\_iter}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{\_\_generic\_file\_write\_iter()}{\_\_generic\_file\_write\_iter()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_a635dc9ed21bd0472d442fa2ec051f4ce}{ssize\+\_\+t}} \+\_\+\+\_\+generic\+\_\+file\+\_\+write\+\_\+iter (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structkiocb}{kiocb}} $\ast$}]{iocb,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structiov__iter}{iov\+\_\+iter}} $\ast$}]{from }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

\+\_\+\+\_\+generic\+\_\+file\+\_\+write\+\_\+iter -\/ write data to a file @iocb\+: IO state structure (file, offset, etc.) @from\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{structiov__iter}{iov\+\_\+iter}} with data to write

This function does all the work needed for actually writing data to a file. It does all basic checks, removes S\+U\+ID from the file, updates modification times and calls proper subroutines depending on whether we do direct IO or a standard buffered write.

It expects i\+\_\+rwsem to be grabbed unless we work on a block device or similar object which does not need locking at all.

This function does {\itshape not} take care of syncing data in case of O\+\_\+\+S\+Y\+NC write. A caller has to handle it. This is mainly due to the fact that we want to avoid syncing under i\+\_\+rwsem.

Return\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item number of bytes written, even for truncated writes
\item negative error code if no data has been written at all 
\end{DoxyItemize}Here is the call graph for this function\+:

filemap\+\_\+check\+\_\+wb\+\_\+err -\/ has an error occurred since the mark was sampled? @mapping\+: mapping to check for writeback errors \begin{DoxySince}{Since}
\+: previously-\/sampled errseq\+\_\+t
\end{DoxySince}
Grab the errseq\+\_\+t value from the mapping, and see if it has changed \char`\"{}since\char`\"{} the given value was sampled.

If it has then report the latest error set, otherwise return 0. \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a4d09b90cb50f8ea6e653e56f85bf93c0}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a4d09b90cb50f8ea6e653e56f85bf93c0}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{filemap\_fdatawait\_keep\_errors()}{filemap\_fdatawait\_keep\_errors()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}} filemap\+\_\+fdatawait\+\_\+keep\+\_\+errors (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} $\ast$}]{mapping }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

filemap\+\_\+fdatawait\+\_\+keep\+\_\+errors -\/ wait for writeback without clearing errors @mapping\+: address space structure to wait for

Walk the list of under-\/writeback pages of the given address space and wait for all of them. Unlike \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_a4d09b90cb50f8ea6e653e56f85bf93c0}{filemap\+\_\+fdatawait()}}, this function does not clear error status of the address space.

Use this function if callers don\textquotesingle{}t handle errors themselves. Expected call sites are system-\/wide / filesystem-\/wide data flushers\+: e.\+g. sync(2), fsfreeze(8)

Return\+: error status of the address space. Here is the call graph for this function\+:
% FIG 45
\mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a9335ba8459ef700817b5c99bac63647a}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a9335ba8459ef700817b5c99bac63647a}}

filemap\+\_\+fdatawait\+\_\+range -\/ wait for writeback to complete @mapping\+: address space structure to wait for @start\+\_\+byte\+: offset in bytes where the range starts @end\+\_\+byte\+: offset in bytes where the range ends (inclusive)

Walk the list of under-\/writeback pages of the given address space in the given range and wait for all of them. Check error status of the address space and return it.

Since the error status of the address space is cleared by this function, callers are responsible for checking the return value and handling and/or reporting the error.

Return\+: error status of the address space. Here is the call graph for this function\+:

filemap\+\_\+sample\+\_\+wb\+\_\+err -\/ sample the current errseq\+\_\+t to test for later errors @mapping\+: mapping to be sampled

Writeback errors are always reported relative to a particular sample point in the past. This function provides those sample points. \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a8ff0e6fdfc662f9b776c9c238dfd066b}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a8ff0e6fdfc662f9b776c9c238dfd066b}}

{\footnotesize\ttfamily static void filemap\+\_\+set\+\_\+wb\+\_\+err (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} $\ast$}]{mapping,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{dcache_8h_ad65b480f8c8270356b45a9890f6499ae}{int}}}]{err }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

filemap\+\_\+set\+\_\+wb\+\_\+err -\/ set a writeback error on an \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} @mapping\+: mapping in which to set writeback error @err\+: error to be set in mapping

When writeback fails in some way, we must record that error so that userspace can be informed when fsync and the like are called. We endeavor to report errors on any file that was open at the time of the error. Some internal callers also need to know when writeback errors have occurred.

When a writeback error occurs, most filesystems will want to call filemap\+\_\+set\+\_\+wb\+\_\+err to record the error in the mapping so that it will be automatically reported whenever fsync is called on the file. \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a00c71aaf64564e557d67aa1b5cbe4cd4}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a00c71aaf64564e557d67aa1b5cbe4cd4}}

filemap\+\_\+write\+\_\+and\+\_\+wait\+\_\+range -\/ write out \& wait on a file range @mapping\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} for the pages @lstart\+: offset in bytes where the range starts @lend\+: offset in bytes where the range ends (inclusive)

Write out and wait upon file offsets lstart-\/$>$lend, inclusive.

Note that @lend is inclusive (describes the last byte to be written) so that this function can be used to write to the very end-\/of-\/file (end = -\/1).

\mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_ae909422aa5e796544c7021159e8ac3ce}{fsuidgid\+\_\+has\+\_\+mapping()}} -\/ check whether caller\textquotesingle{}s fsuid/fsgid is mapped @sb\+: the superblock we want a mapping in @mnt\+\_\+userns\+: user namespace of the relevant mount

Check whether the caller\textquotesingle{}s fsuid and fsgid have a valid mapping in the s\+\_\+user\+\_\+ns of the superblock @sb. If the caller is on an idmapped mount map the caller\textquotesingle{}s fsuid and fsgid according to the @mnt\+\_\+userns first.

\index{generic\_file\_read\_iter@{generic\_file\_read\_iter}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{generic\_file\_read\_iter()}{generic\_file\_read\_iter()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_a635dc9ed21bd0472d442fa2ec051f4ce}{ssize\+\_\+t}} generic\+\_\+file\+\_\+read\+\_\+iter (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structkiocb}{kiocb}} $\ast$}]{iocb,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structiov__iter}{iov\+\_\+iter}} $\ast$}]{iter }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

generic\+\_\+file\+\_\+read\+\_\+iter -\/ generic filesystem read routine @iocb\+: kernel I/O control block @iter\+: destination for the data read

This is the \char`\"{}read\+\_\+iter()\char`\"{} routine for all filesystems that can use the page cache directly.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} I\+O\+C\+B\+\_\+\+N\+O\+W\+A\+IT flag in iocb-\/$>$ki\+\_\+flags indicates that -\/E\+A\+G\+A\+IN shall be returned when no data can be read without waiting for I/O requests to complete; it doesn\textquotesingle{}t prevent readahead.

\mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} I\+O\+C\+B\+\_\+\+N\+O\+IO flag in iocb-\/$>$ki\+\_\+flags indicates that no new I/O requests shall be made for the read or for readahead. When no data can be read, -\/E\+A\+G\+A\+IN shall be returned. When readahead would be triggered, a partial, possibly empty read shall be returned.

Return\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item number of bytes copied, even for partial reads
\item negative error code (or 0 if I\+O\+C\+B\+\_\+\+N\+O\+IO) if nothing was read 
\end{DoxyItemize}Here is the call graph for this function\+:

generic\+\_\+file\+\_\+write\+\_\+iter -\/ write data to a file @iocb\+: IO state structure @from\+: \mbox{\hyperlink{structiov__iter}{iov\+\_\+iter}} with data to write

This is a wrapper around \mbox{\hyperlink{filemap_8c_afcc4a74ba046067e2df44e8a3875199c}{\+\_\+\+\_\+generic\+\_\+file\+\_\+write\+\_\+iter()}} to be used by most filesystems. It takes care of syncing the file in case of O\+\_\+\+S\+Y\+NC file and acquires i\+\_\+rwsem as needed. Return\+:
\begin{DoxyItemize}
\item negative error code if no data has been written at all of \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_ae306f1702cb8dc8c79c3388afc632a4f}{vfs\+\_\+fsync\+\_\+range()}} failed for a synchronous write
\item number of bytes written, even for truncated writes 
\end{DoxyItemize}Here is the call graph for this function\+:

i\+\_\+gid\+\_\+into\+\_\+mnt -\/ map an inode\textquotesingle{}s i\+\_\+gid down into a mnt\+\_\+userns @mnt\+\_\+userns\+: user namespace of the mount the inode was found from @inode\+: inode to map

Return\+: the inode\textquotesingle{}s i\+\_\+gid mapped down according to @mnt\+\_\+userns. If the inode\textquotesingle{}s i\+\_\+gid has no mapping I\+N\+V\+A\+L\+I\+D\+\_\+\+G\+ID is returned. Here is the caller graph for this function\+:

{\footnotesize\ttfamily static void inode\+\_\+fsuid\+\_\+set (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structinode}{inode}} $\ast$}]{inode,  }\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structuser__namespace}{user\+\_\+namespace}} $\ast$}]{mnt\+\_\+userns }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

inode\+\_\+fsuid\+\_\+set -\/ initialize inode\textquotesingle{}s i\+\_\+uid field with callers fsuid @inode\+: inode to initialize @mnt\+\_\+userns\+: user namespace of the mount the inode was found from

Initialize the i\+\_\+uid field of @inode. If the inode was found/created via an idmapped mount map the caller\textquotesingle{}s fsuid according to @mnt\+\_\+users. \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_af51351c35bfe6e13ddd4351a02d97f43}\label{linux_2fs_8h_af51351c35bfe6e13ddd4351a02d97f43}}

invalidate\+\_\+inode\+\_\+pages2 -\/ remove all pages from an \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} @mapping\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}}

Any pages which are found to be mapped into pagetables are unmapped prior to invalidation.

invalidate\+\_\+inode\+\_\+pages2\+\_\+range -\/ remove range of pages from an \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} @mapping\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} @start\+: the page offset \textquotesingle{}from\textquotesingle{} which to invalidate @end\+: the page offset \textquotesingle{}to\textquotesingle{} which to invalidate (inclusive)

\index{invalidate\_mapping\_pages@{invalidate\_mapping\_pages}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{invalidate\_mapping\_pages()}{invalidate\_mapping\_pages()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily unsigned long invalidate\+\_\+mapping\+\_\+pages (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} $\ast$}]{mapping,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_ae3c121c712f5512be1aa352b15942649}{pgoff\+\_\+t}}}]{start,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_ae3c121c712f5512be1aa352b15942649}{pgoff\+\_\+t}}}]{end }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

invalidate\+\_\+mapping\+\_\+pages -\/ Invalidate all clean, unlocked cache of one inode @mapping\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} which holds the cache to invalidate @start\+: the offset \textquotesingle{}from\textquotesingle{} which to invalidate @end\+: the offset \textquotesingle{}to\textquotesingle{} which to invalidate (inclusive)

This function removes pages that are clean, unmapped and unlocked, as well as shadow entries. It will not block on IO activity.

If you want to remove all the pages of one inode, regardless of their use and writeback state, use \mbox{\hyperlink{truncate_8c_adccecbaab0550d992f959ce8488802a1}{truncate\+\_\+inode\+\_\+pages()}}.

Return\+: the number of the cache entries that were invalidated Here is the call graph for this function\+:

\mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a0586d5c6e4eac9ac088f98fde266e9b8}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a0586d5c6e4eac9ac088f98fde266e9b8}}

\index{invalidate\_mapping\_pagevec@{invalidate\_mapping\_pagevec}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{invalidate\_mapping\_pagevec()}{invalidate\_mapping\_pagevec()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily void invalidate\+\_\+mapping\+\_\+pagevec (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} $\ast$}]{mapping,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_ae3c121c712f5512be1aa352b15942649}{pgoff\+\_\+t}}}]{start,  }\item[{\mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_ae3c121c712f5512be1aa352b15942649}{pgoff\+\_\+t}}}]{end,  }\item[{unsigned long $\ast$}]{nr\+\_\+pagevec }\end{DoxyParamCaption})}

invalidate\+\_\+mapping\+\_\+pagevec -\/ Invalidate all the unlocked pages of one inode @mapping\+: the \mbox{\hyperlink{structaddress__space}{address\+\_\+space}} which holds the pages to invalidate @start\+: the offset \textquotesingle{}from\textquotesingle{} which to invalidate @end\+: the offset \textquotesingle{}to\textquotesingle{} which to invalidate (inclusive) @nr\+\_\+pagevec\+: invalidate failed page number for caller

This helper is similar to \mbox{\hyperlink{truncate_8c_a9970b944654dccb6e99dcfc93d03bce7}{invalidate\+\_\+mapping\+\_\+pages()}}, except that it accounts for pages that are likely on a pagevec and counts them in @nr\+\_\+pagevec, which will be used by the caller. mapped\+\_\+fsgid -\/ return caller\textquotesingle{}s fsgid mapped up into a mnt\+\_\+userns @mnt\+\_\+userns\+: user namespace of the relevant mount

Use this helper to initialize a new vfs or filesystem object based on the caller\textquotesingle{}s fsgid. A common example is initializing the i\+\_\+gid field of a newly allocated inode triggered by a creation event such as mkdir or O\+\_\+\+C\+R\+E\+AT. Other examples include the allocation of quotas for a specific user.

Return\+: the caller\textquotesingle{}s current fsgid mapped up according to @mnt\+\_\+userns. \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_ae96be245af09c6163af74b11298467bf}\label{linux_2fs_8h_ae96be245af09c6163af74b11298467bf}}

mapped\+\_\+fsuid -\/ return caller\textquotesingle{}s fsuid mapped up into a mnt\+\_\+userns @mnt\+\_\+userns\+: user namespace of the relevant mount

Use this helper to initialize a new vfs or filesystem object based on the caller\textquotesingle{}s fsuid. A common example is initializing the i\+\_\+uid field of a newly allocated inode triggered by a creation event such as mkdir or O\+\_\+\+C\+R\+E\+AT. Other examples include the allocation of quotas for a specific user.

Return\+: the caller\textquotesingle{}s current fsuid mapped up according to @mnt\+\_\+userns. \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_ab1028482650e09305a05d5a4756a04bd}\label{linux_2fs_8h_ab1028482650e09305a05d5a4756a04bd}}

\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{sb\_start\_intwrite()}{sb\_start\_intwrite()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static void sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+intwrite (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structsuper__block}{super\+\_\+block}} $\ast$}]{sb }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+intwrite -\/ get write access to a superblock for internal fs purposes @sb\+: the super we write to

This is the third level of protection against filesystem freezing. It is free for use by a filesystem. \mbox{\hyperlink{classThe}{The}} only requirement is that it must rank below sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+pagefault.

For example filesystem can call \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_a7089966684233f200dfc9ee9fa7230d7}{sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+intwrite()}} when starting a transaction which somewhat eases handling of freezing for internal sources of filesystem changes (internal fs threads, discarding preallocation on file close, etc.). \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_acd32769e9087b7e9f0387e193f55f395}\label{linux_2fs_8h_acd32769e9087b7e9f0387e193f55f395}} 
\index{fs.h@{fs.h}!sb\_start\_intwrite\_trylock@{sb\_start\_intwrite\_trylock}}
\index{sb\_start\_intwrite\_trylock@{sb\_start\_intwrite\_trylock}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{sb\_start\_intwrite\_trylock()}{sb\_start\_intwrite\_trylock()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static \mbox{\hyperlink{include_2linux_2types_8h_afaa87723b8417d40fcf45b7330261ef9}{bool}} sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+intwrite\+\_\+trylock (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structsuper__block}{super\+\_\+block}} $\ast$}]{sb }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

\mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a5433d6536d71b06788279d0e8c702e1a}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a5433d6536d71b06788279d0e8c702e1a}} 
\index{fs.h@{fs.h}!sb\_start\_pagefault@{sb\_start\_pagefault}}
\index{sb\_start\_pagefault@{sb\_start\_pagefault}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{sb\_start\_pagefault()}{sb\_start\_pagefault()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static void sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+pagefault (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structsuper__block}{super\+\_\+block}} $\ast$}]{sb }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+pagefault -\/ get write access to a superblock from a page fault @sb\+: the super we write to

When a process starts handling write page fault, it should embed the operation into \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_a5433d6536d71b06788279d0e8c702e1a}{sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+pagefault()}} -\/ \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_a7fb58425edd5e58263286596525e4f1e}{sb\+\_\+end\+\_\+pagefault()}} pair to get exclusion against file system freezing. This is needed since the page fault is going to dirty a page. This function increments number of running page faults preventing freezing. If the file system is already frozen, the function waits until the file system is thawed.

Since page fault freeze protection behaves as a lock, users have to preserve ordering of freeze protection and other filesystem locks. It is advised to put \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_a5433d6536d71b06788279d0e8c702e1a}{sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+pagefault()}} close to mmap\+\_\+lock in lock ordering. Page fault handling code implies lock dependency\+:

mmap\+\_\+lock -\/$>$ sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+pagefault \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a58045e0c72b4e8d0599e214b8ce6090a}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a58045e0c72b4e8d0599e214b8ce6090a}} 
\index{fs.h@{fs.h}!sb\_start\_write@{sb\_start\_write}}
\index{sb\_start\_write@{sb\_start\_write}!fs.h@{fs.h}}
\doxysubsubsection{\texorpdfstring{sb\_start\_write()}{sb\_start\_write()}}
{\footnotesize\ttfamily static void sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+write (\begin{DoxyParamCaption}\item[{struct \mbox{\hyperlink{structsuper__block}{super\+\_\+block}} $\ast$}]{sb }\end{DoxyParamCaption})\hspace{0.3cm}{\ttfamily [inline]}, {\ttfamily [static]}}

sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+write -\/ get write access to a superblock @sb\+: the super we write to

When a process wants to write data or metadata to a file system (i.\+e. dirty a page or an inode), it should embed the operation in a \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_a58045e0c72b4e8d0599e214b8ce6090a}{sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+write()}} -\/ \mbox{\hyperlink{linux_2fs_8h_a6d36fe094e46bb90df6cbea6dbe658c0}{sb\+\_\+end\+\_\+write()}} pair to get exclusion against file system freezing. This function increments number of writers preventing freezing. If the file system is already frozen, the function waits until the file system is thawed.

Since freeze protection behaves as a lock, users have to preserve ordering of freeze protection and other filesystem locks. Generally, freeze protection should be the outermost lock. In particular, we have\+:

sb\+\_\+start\+\_\+write -\/$>$ i\+\_\+mutex (write path, truncate, directory ops, ...) -\/$>$ s\+\_\+umount (freeze\+\_\+super, thaw\+\_\+super) \mbox{\Hypertarget{linux_2fs_8h_a70e3f3632e60438c3cf0700e13ed009c}\label{linux_2fs_8h_a70e3f3632e60438c3cf0700e13ed009c}}
